Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは5年に1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が67％を占めていた（資料1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、 「安全運用が基本」とした（注1） 。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。 「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注3）。GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、 「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、 「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、 「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである4資産を25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、 「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注11） 。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に3％台に達し、約30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、 「国内債券の利回りが3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注12）。 
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注13）。2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

improving commodity DRAM volume and better momentum from driver IC due to OLED

iPhone ramp  (27% of DDIC sales, +46% QoQ), while it also raised memory backend ASP
to reflect rising component cost. Q326 GM improved to 21.7% (+3.7ppt QoQ) on better
pricing, lower pass through for bumping and higher loading, and  exceeded our estimate
of 18.6%. Q326 OPEX remains well controlled at 6.4% of sales, while it also booked
one-off other operating income of NT$373mn, resulting in better OPM of 19.7%.
Memory and OLED momentum to sustain into Q426

ChipMOS sees better momentum for memory over driver IC business into Q426F  on
continued strength from commodity DRAM and improving NAND Flash outlook. For its
DDIC/bumping business, despite softer end-demand for consumer electronics,
particularly PC and Android smartphone, ChipMOS noted the seasonal build for OLED
iPhone should provide some cushion for its driver IC sales. Management noted it will
continue to optimize its product portfolio and divest lower-end capacity, while

negotiates with its customers on pass through higher component cost if needed. We
forecast ChipMOS's Q426E sales to grow 2% QoQ, with GM expanding by 0.3ppt QoQ
to 22.0% on better  product mix and normalized electricity cost.
Optics and AI ASIC testing support 2027E growth
Management noted its copper bumping for US customers' next-generation products
remain under qualification and expects to see more meaningful revenue contribution by
late-2027F or 2028F. In the meantime, it is  working with the customer on producing

some legacy products in 2027F, although revenue contribution remains limited. As
optics-related business carries significantly better pricing than traditional DDIC
bumping, ChipMOS will prioritize its driver IC/bumping resource for optics and OLED
DDI, instead for Korean customer's outsourcing. Separately, ChipMOS continues to
expand its ASIC testing roadmap, having already entered mass production for
smartphone AP and expecting to progress to smartphone SoC testing, followed by ASIC

final test opportunities if the current program ramps successfully through H127.
Valuation: Reiterate Buy rating with price target of NT$155
We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 6-23% on  improving GM profile driven by more favorable
mix but trim our PT from NT$166 to NT$155 as we apply a lower multiple of 20x 2027-
28E average PE (from 23x) given the recent market valuation contraction. Reiterate Buy
rating on improving margin profiles new AI (optics and logic testing) initiatives.

Company Description
ChipMOS is a semiconductor back-end supplier with 45-50% of its sales from display driver
IC (~40% from TV and 40% from smartphone and IT and 20% from automotive), 35-40%
from memory (~40% DRAM, ~40% from specialty flash and ~20% from mainstream NAND)
and 10-15% from logic (TV SoC) and mixed signal IC (MEMS, PMIC).

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target for ChipMOS is based on a target PE multiple. Downside risks include a
weaker-than-expected tech end-market demand recovery, growing China competition,
failure to diversify into the SiPh/optical component bumping business and a slower ramp of its
logic testing business.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

SK하이닉스 (000660)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 3,200,000원

Document 238: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

작성자(이선경)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

목표주가 2,000,000

(26.10.02) 
1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

Document 289: Anthropic IPO估值1.3万亿美元，创始人人均身家180亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic IPO估值1.3万亿美元，创始人人均身家180亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

七位创始⼈各⾃计划在 IPO 中套现约 2.5 亿美元，出售约 340 万股。上市后，每位创始⼈仍
将持有公司约 3.2% 的 A 类股份，按发⾏价区间中点计算，对应市值约为 180 亿美元，七位创
始⼈合计持股⽐例约为 22% ，但是通过特殊架构牢牢掌控 50.1% 的绝对投票权。
摘要：
Anthropic 正以硅⾕近年来规模最⼤的 IPO 之⼀冲击公开市场，七位联合创始⼈将在上市后各⾃
坐拥约 180 亿美元⾝家，彰显这家 AI 公司⾃ 2021 年成⽴以来的惊⼈增⻓速度。
据华尔街⽇报报道， Anthropic 周⼆（ 11 ⽉ 10 ⽇）提交的⽂件显⽰，Anthropic 计划最快下周上
市，⽬标估值约 1.3 万亿美元，拟通过股票发⾏及向⾕歌等⼤型投资者定向配售相结合的⽅式，
募资约 800 亿美元或更多。 此次 IPO 发⾏价区间定为每股 70 ⾄ 78 美元。
七位创始⼈各⾃计划在 IPO 中套现约 2.5 亿美元，出售约 340 万股。上市后，每位创始⼈仍将持有
公司约 3.2% 的 A 类股份，按发⾏价区间中点计算，对应市值约为 180 亿美元，七位创始⼈合计持

股⽐例约为 22%。
据 PitchBook 数据， Anthropic 在今年 5 ⽉的融资轮中估值约为 9650 亿美元，此次 IPO ⽬标估值较
彼时进⼀步⼤幅跃升。
七位创始⼈：从 OpenAI 出⾛到千亿帝国
Anthropic 的七位联合创始⼈均将在此次 IPO 中实现财富的历史性跨越。⾸席执⾏官 Dario
Amodei 与其姐姐、公司总裁兼董事会主席 Daniela Amodei 是最⼴为⼈知的两位创始⼈。其余
五位分别为：
根据公司⽂件，七位创始⼈在 IPO 前各持有 Anthropic 有投票权普通股的 3.6% ⾄ 3.7% 。上市后，
每⼈仍将持有约 3.2% 的 A 类股份，七⼈合计持股约 22% 。
⾸席计算官 Tom Brown 、⾸席科学官 Jared Kaplan 、⾸席架构师 Sam McCandlish 、负责公司内
部 AI 安全智库 Anthropic Institute 的 Jack Clark ，以及主导可解释性研究的 Christopher Olah 。

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
36 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
复杂股权架构：创始⼈掌握 50.1% 投票权
尽管七位创始⼈合计持有约 22% 的流通股，但得益于 Anthropic 独特⽽复杂的资本结构，他们实
际掌握的投票权远超持股⽐例。通过⼀家由他们共同控制的有限责任公司，七位创始⼈被保证
在绝⼤多数股东投票事项中合计⾏使 50.1% 的投票权。
Anthropic 表⽰，这 50.1% 的投票权将按创始⼈多数意⻅统⼀⾏使，但每位创始⼈仍可就其个⼈
持有的 A 类股份单独投票。
在董事会层⾯，创始⼈的控制权同样举⾜轻重 —— 他们有权提名接近半数的董事席位。董事会
的多数席位则由 Anthropic 设⽴的⼀个特殊信托机构负责遴选，该信托与公司管理层及员⼯协同
运作。
此次 IPO 的规模与速度，集中体现了 AI ⾏业的资本狂热。Anthropic 于 2021 年成⽴，距今不⾜四

年，⽬标估值已达 1.3 万亿美元。据 PitchBook 数据，仅在今年 5 ⽉的融资轮中，公司估值还约为
9650 亿美元，此次 IPO ⽬标估值较之⼜⼤幅提升逾三成。

Document 328: CoreWeave财报背后：AI资本开支模式出现重大迁移

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave财报背后：AI资本开支模式出现重大迁移.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

CoreWeave 单季 CapEx ⾼达 145 亿美元，债务近 470 亿，净亏损 7 亿 —— 但真正的变局在
于： AI 算⼒融资模式正从云⼚商单独举债，转向客⼾预付款（未来三个⽉预计到账 90 亿）、
合同抵押融资、 GPU 资产循环租赁多轨并⾏。更激进的是， Omni 模式让客⼾⾃购 GPU 、
CoreWeave 只卖运营服务，资本⻛险开始向需求端转移。这是 AI 投资从 " 叙事驱动 " 迈向 " 资
本回报验证 " 的关键分⽔岭。
摘要：
在⼀⽚悲观声中， CoreWeave 发了新季度的财报，本次 CoreWeave 财报最值得研究的，不是收
⼊增⻓ 168% ，⽽是 AI 基础设施的资本承担⽅式开始从单⼀的云⼚商举债扩张，转向客⼾预付
款、合同担保融资、经营现⾦流及资本市场融资共同⽀撑的结构。
对于 neocloud 公司来说，融资压⼒可能会明显降低，前阵⼦市场如此悲观的⼀个核⼼催化剂就
是他的 CDS 飙升，然后 poly 上有⼤量赌注是赌他 27 年破产。
我的初步判断是：

资本来源发⽣了明显变化。  客⼾不再仅仅在未来按⽉⽀付算⼒服务费，⽽是越来越多地参与
早期资⾦筹措，并通过预付、押⾦及⻓期采购合同降低 CoreWeave 的部分融资⻛险。
资本所有权尚未发⽣同等程度的变化。  客⼾提前付款，不代表客⼾取得 GPU 所有权，也不意
味着 GPU 采购、折旧、融资成本与残值⻛险已经全部转移给客⼾。
新的融资模式仍存在重⼤尾部⻛险。  CoreWeave 借款规模继续扩⼤。其 Q3 净利息费⽤达到
8.46 亿美元，超过 2.07 亿美元的 GAAP 营业利润。业务在运营层⾯实现盈利，但财务杠杆尚未
实现经济意义上的安全闭环。
谁在为 AI 买单？ CoreWeave 财报揭开⼀场正在发⽣的资本⾰命
—— 从巨额举债购买 GPU ，到客⼾预付款、合同信⽤与算⼒资产循环， AI CapEx 正在进⼊新阶
段。
2026 年 11 ⽉ 10 ⽇， CoreWeave 发布了⼀份极端悲观情形下的⼜超预期的财报。第三季度收⼊
36.61 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 168% ；在⼿收⼊合同积压达到 1325 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 138% ；单季度资

本开⽀⾼达 145 亿美元，全年 CapEx 指引进⼀步提⾼⾄ 370 亿 —390 亿美元。
这些数字已经⾜够惊⼈。
但在我看来，这份财报最值得研究的，不是收⼊增⻓ 168% ，⽽是 AI 基础设施的融资模式正在发
⽣深刻变化。过去⼏年， AI 基础设施扩张的核⼼逻辑相对简单：云计算公司融资，购买英伟达
GPU ，建设数据中⼼，然后出租算⼒，通过未来客⼾收⼊回收投资。
这是⼀种典型的资本密集型扩张模式。收⼊增⻓得越快，往往意味着需要投⼊更多资本，形成
更多债务，承担更⾼的融资成本。
但这套模式正在演化。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

CoreWeave 披露，未来约三个⽉预计获得 90 亿美元客⼾预付款及押⾦，部分⻓期客⼾合同已经
能够⽀持相关资本开⽀ 70%—100% 的资产级融资，甚⾄出现了客⼾⾃⼰拥有 GPU 、由
CoreWeave 提供运营平台的新模式。
⼀场从 “ 企业⾃⼰融资建设算⼒ ” ，向 “ 客⼾需求直接参与资本形成 ” 的转变，正在发⽣。这可能是
AI 投资周期进⼊第⼆阶段的重要信号。
以后融资的模式包括，⼤客⼾要⽤ AI ，我不承担买卡成本，你要⽤就⾃⼰买，你⾃⼰承担这个开
⽀。
⼀、 AI 基础设施最⼤的瓶颈，正在从技术转向资本
过去三年， AI 基础设施的扩张速度⼏乎超出了所有传统产业周期的经验。
GPU 越来越贵，集群越来越⼤，电⼒和⼟地成为稀缺资源，数据中⼼建设周期不断拉⻓。
真正的问题逐渐显现：即使未来有⼤量客⼾愿意购买算⼒，谁来承担今天动辄数⼗亿美元的建
设成本？
CoreWeave 很好地展⽰了这⼀⽭盾。
2026 年第三季度，公司实现营业利润 2.07 亿美元，但同期净利息费⽤达到 8.46 亿美元，最终净

亏损 7.09 亿美元。截⾄ 9 ⽉底，公司有息债务合计约 468.7 亿美元，较 2025 年底显著增加。更值
得注意的是，虽然第三季度经营现⾦流为 19.75 亿美元，但现⾦购买固定资产及资本化软件⽀出
达到 94.41 亿美元。简单相减，现⾦流缺⼝约 74.66 亿美元。
这还不包括所有其他投资活动，也不等同于公司官⽅定义的⾃由现⾦流。
如此庞⼤的资⾦需求，意味着即使业务增⻓迅猛，资本市场也可能成为公司扩张的真正约束。
传统认知认为， AI 基础设施的核⼼⻛险是需求不⾜。
现在我们需要加⼊另⼀个问题：即使需求真实存在，如果融资成本持续上升，企业还能否以⾜
够⾼的资本回报率满⾜这些需求？
CoreWeave 开始给出新的答案。
⼆、第⼀项变化：客⼾合同正在成为融资抵押品
CoreWeave 管理层在电话会议中披露，未来将继续主要通过与特定客⼾合同相匹配的延迟提款
定期贷款（ DDTL ），为新增基础设施建设融资。
这些贷款可以覆盖相关 CapEx 的 70%—100% 。
⾃上市以来，此类贷款的定价⼤致为 SOFR 加 225—550 个基点，优质投资级客⼾对应的项⽬通

常处于较低利差区间。管理层还预计，下⼀项投资级项⽬融资的收益率约为 7%—7.5% ，能够覆
盖超过 90% 的相关资本开⽀。
这⾥发⽣了⾮常重要的变化。

### Page 3

传统企业融资主要依赖融资主体⾃⾝的信⽤能⼒、资产负债表和经营现⾦流。但在合同⽀持的
项⽬融资结构中，贷款机构可以进⼀步评估未来算⼒购买合同、客⼾信⽤质量、 GPU 抵押品，
以及项⽬本⾝的偿债现⾦流。
假设⼀个 GPU 基础设施项⽬需要投资 100 亿美元。
在传统模式下，云服务商可能需要通过股权、公司债券或其他融资⼯具筹集⼤部分资⾦。
⽽在合同⽀持融资模式下，如果项⽬取得 80% 的贷款覆盖率，公司就可以通过项⽬贷款筹措 80
亿美元，剩余 20 亿美元再由其他资本来源⽀持。
关键在于，银⾏不仅在评估 CoreWeave ，还在评估对应客⼾能否持续履⾏⻓期算⼒购买义务。
未来算⼒需求因此不仅是收⼊预测，更成为今天获取资本的信⽤基础。
但必须强调：客⼾合同⽀持融资，不代表客⼾承担了全部债务责任。贷款的法律追索范围、抵
押结构和现⾦流分配，仍取决于具体契约。
这也不是 CoreWeave 刚刚发明的模式。真正的新变化，是这种合同⽀持融资正在 AI 基础设施扩
张中变得更加重要，规模也越来越⼤。

三、第⼆项变化：客⼾开始提前付款，直接参与资本形成
如果说合同融资改变了银⾏如何放贷，那么客⼾预付款则进⼀步改变了资⾦流动的时间顺序。
CoreWeave 披露， 2026 年⾄今，约 70% 的签约交易包含客⼾预付款安排，部分客⼾甚⾄提前⽀
付⾼达合同总价值 100% 的资⾦。
未来约三个⽉，公司预计从已签客⼾合同中收到约 90 亿美元预付款和押⾦。
注意两个关键区别。
第⼀， 70% 是交易数量⼝径，不代表 70% 的总 CapEx 已经由客⼾⽀付。
第⼆，⽀付 100% 的合同⾦额，也不等于客⼾直接购买了全部 GPU 设备。
这类预付款通常仍然对应未来需要履⾏的算⼒服务义务，并⾮收款时⽴即实现的利润。
但它的融资意义不可低估。
过去的现⾦流顺序是：企业先融资、买 GPU 、建设数据中⼼，客⼾以后逐⽉付款。现在，部分
项⽬可以先取得客⼾现⾦，再⽤于⽀持后续基础设施建设和服务交付。客⼾的未来⽀出预算，
开始提前转化为整个 AI 基础设施体系的当前资本供给。
这意味着融资⻛险正在重新分配。

云服务商可能减少部分外部融资需求，客⼾则承担更⼤的前期现⾦投⼊、资⾦占⽤和⻓期算⼒
承诺。银⾏、云服务商与 AI 客⼾之间的关系，也从简单的购买和销售，演变为⼀种更深层次的资
本协作。

### Page 4

当然，这不是免费的融资。 CoreWeave 管理层明确表⽰，会⽐较客⼾预付款带来的定价影响与
其他融资⽅式的成本，选择经济上更有吸引⼒的⽅案。
如果客⼾要求较⼤折扣，预付款虽然改善流动性，却可能压低项⽬的⻓期利润率。所以真正应
该⽐较的是：预付款降低的融资成本，能否超过因此付出的价格折让及其他合同成本。
四、第三项变化： GPU 不再只是⼀次性投资，⽽可能成为循环产⽣现⾦流
的资产
这次电话会议还有⼀个容易被忽视，却可能影响 AI 基础设施⻓期估值的细节。
在预计收到的 90 亿美元客⼾预付款及押⾦中，超过 30 亿美元与已有算⼒基础设施的重新签约有
关。这些基础设施不需要再次投⼊原始 GPU 采购成本。
CoreWeave 管理层指出，公司已经通过相关融资机制偿还超过 40 亿美元债务。对于采⽤相应⻓
期融资结构的项⽬，初始客⼾合同结束时，债务可以完成偿还，⽽ GPU 资产仍由公司持有。
之后，这些 GPU 可以再次出租。这意味着 AI 硬件可能拥有第⼆轮、甚⾄第三轮经济⽣命周期。
管理层预计， 2027 年将有超过 13 万块 GPU 结束初始合同， 2028 年另有约 17 万块。以当前定价估

算， 2027 年相关续约机会对应接近 40 亿美元的潜在年化收⼊；包括 2028 年到期设备在内，潜在
年化收⼊机会接近 100 亿美元。这些数字不是已经签订的新增合同，也尚未纳⼊公司现有
Backlog ，只是管理层依据当前价格作出的机会估算。
但它揭⽰出⼀项重要的资本回报机制：如果第⼀轮合同已经覆盖设备成本和融资偿还，后续续
租收⼊就可能具有明显不同的单位经济性。

### Page 5

这有点类似航空租赁、设备租赁和基础设施运营。
第⼀轮资产投资需要承担最⾼的资⾦成本；随着融资偿还，资产进⼊后续经营周期，剩余经济
价值可能开始显现。
当然， GPU 与⻜机、电站并不完全⼀样。 GPU 性能迭代更快，技术淘汰和租赁价格下降⻛险更
⾼。旧设备也仍然需要电⼒、运维及必要的更新投⼊。
所以未来最关键的，不只是 GPU 能够使⽤多少年，⽽是：第⼀轮合同之后， GPU 还能以什么价
格、什么利⽤率、多少额外成本持续创造现⾦流？
这将直接决定 AI 算⼒资产应当如何估值。
五、第四项变化：客⼾开始拥有 GPU ，云平台向资产轻模式延伸
如果说前⾯三项变化仍然建⽴在 CoreWeave 持有基础设施的模式上，那么 CoreWeave Omni 则
触及了更深层次的转型。
按照管理层披露， Omni 可以把 CoreWeave 的软件平台与运营能⼒延伸⾄客⼾或第三⽅拥有的基
础设施。
客⼾保留底层 GPU 等基础设施的所有权， CoreWeave 不必⾃⾏融资购买相关设备，也能通过提
供平台能⼒和运营服务创造收⼊。

公司已经签署⾸个 Omni 客⼾。这是真正意义上的资本承担主体变化。
在传统云计算模式中，平台公司既承担基础设施资本开⽀，也提供软件和运营服务。
⽽ Omni 尝试把两者分开：拥有资本的客⼾或第三⽅购买设备，专业平台负责部署、调度、管理
和优化算⼒。
如果这种模式形成规模， CoreWeave 就有机会从⾼度资本密集型云基础设施公司，部分转向软
件与运营能⼒输出平台。但⽬前仅披露⾸个客⼾，还没有⾜够证据证明它能够形成可观收⼊或
改变公司的整体资本结构。
因此，不能因为 Omni 出现，就宣称 AI 产业已经全⾯进⼊客⼾⾃购 GPU 时代。更准确的表述是：
⾃有资产云服务与客⼾⾃有资产的平台运营，开始成为可以并存的两种商业模式。
六、融资创新并不意味着⻛险消失
资本来源多元化，看起来降低了 CoreWeave 的融资压⼒。

### Page 6

但这并不代表整个 AI 产业的⻛险在消失。
它可能只是改变了⻛险的位置。
过去，资本⻛险主要集中在购买 GPU 的云基础设施运营商⾝上。
未来，⻛险可能沿着客⼾预付款、⻓期采购合同、项⽬贷款、设备抵押品和算⼒运营平台，传
递给更多参与者。
尤其值得警惕的是，这些⻛险并不完全独⽴。假设未来 AI 应⽤商业化慢于预期，⼀些模型实验室
及 AI 企业现⾦流开始恶化。
⾸先受到影响的，可能是客⼾提前⽀付算⼒费⽤的能⼒。随后是⻓期算⼒合同的信⽤质量。银
⾏可能要求提⾼贷款利差，降低 GPU 资产融资⽐例，或收紧抵押品要求。如果市场同时出现新
旧 GPU 供给增加，算⼒租赁价格下降， GPU 抵押品价值也可能受到冲击。这时，客⼾信⽤、合
同收⼊、融资能⼒和 GPU 资产价值，就可能同时承压。
原本看起来分散到不同主体的⻛险，会重新形成⾼度相关的反馈链条。
从信⽤ —CapEx 传导框架来看，最需要警惕的正是这种反⾝性：客⼾现⾦流恶化  → 预付款减少
→ 合同融资收紧  → CapEx 放缓  → 产业链订单承压  → 资产价格下跌  → 信⽤进⼀步收缩。

因此，客⼾预付和资产级融资可以改善特定公司的资⾦结构，却不能⾃动消除整个产业的信⽤
周期。另⼀个重要事实是， CoreWeave 第三季度 22.56 亿美元的调整后 EBITDA ，看起来极其强
劲，但同期利息费⽤就达到 8.46 亿美元。经营利润、折旧、利息、未来设备替换及资本开⽀，必
须⼀起分析。
⼀个项⽬能够产⽣正向 EBITDA ，不代表它已经为股东创造了正向的全⽣命周期经济利润。
七、 AI 投资周期的下⼀阶段：从 CapEx 增⻓⾛向资本回报验证
过去⼏年，市场对 AI 产业的主要定价逻辑，是不断上调算⼒需求、 GPU 出货量和数据中⼼资本
开⽀预期。
只要订单增⻓，产业链就容易获得估值溢价。但随着⾏业资本投⼊累计到越来越⾼的⽔平，市
场最终会提出三个更严格的问题。
第⼀，谁来融资？
是云服务商、模型实验室、银⾏、设备制造商，还是终端客⼾？
第⼆，谁来承担⻛险？
如果算⼒价格下降、客⼾违约、设备提前淘汰，最终损失停留在哪⼀层资产负债表？
第三，谁能留下利润？
是 GPU 供应商、数据中⼼开发商、算⼒运营平台，还是掌握终端客⼾和应⽤收⼊的企业？

这三个问题将决定下⼀阶段 AI 产业内部的资本迁移。

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
对于英伟达等硬件供应商⽽⾔，融资主体变化本⾝不必然减少 GPU 订单。但如果终端客⼾资⾦
承受能⼒下降，需求依然可能延后。
对于 Neocloud ⽽⾔，能够取得投资级客⼾⻓期合同、低成本项⽬融资和优质资产续租机会的公
司，可能获得明显的竞争优势。
对于 AI 模型和应⽤企业⽽⾔，提前⽀付算⼒费⽤则意味着必须更早证明商业化能⼒，资本压⼒从
基础设施供应商向应⽤需求端转移。
从⻉叶斯投资视⻆来看， CoreWeave 这份财报显著增强了 “ 客⼾⽀持型融资正在扩张 ” 的证据，
但尚未证明 “ 客⼾⾃有 GPU 模式已成为⾏业主流 ” ，更没有证明整个 AI 基础设施投资周期已经实现
可持续的资本回报。
未来真正值得观察的，是预付款能否按期到账、项⽬融资是否持续低成本完成、旧 GPU 能否以
理想价格续租，以及扣除资本成本后有多少现⾦最终归属于股东。

所以， AI 时代最⼤的变化，可能不只是⽣产⼒，⽽是资本组织⽅式，
回头看，每⼀次重⼤技术⾰命，都不只是技术本⾝的⾰命，也是⼀场资本形成机制的变⾰。铁
路需要新的债券和项⽬融资，电⼒需要⻓期基础设施资本，互联⽹催⽣了⻛险资本与平台经
济。
AI 同样如此。
早期，市场需要资本先⾏，押注未来需求。随着客⼾需求逐步显现，⻓期合同、预付款、资产
级贷款和平台运营模式开始共同参与资本形成。这既可能提⾼整个产业的融资效率，也可能把
⻛险转移到过去没有充分承担⻛险的客⼾和⾦融机构⾝上。
CoreWeave 的最新财报，让我们看到这⼀进程正在发⽣。 AI 基础设施的竞争，正在从 “ 谁能买到
最多 GPU” ，逐渐升级为 “ 谁能以最低资本成本组织算⼒，谁能回收最多现⾦流，谁能把技术需求
最终转化为可持续的股东经济利润 ” 。
这可能是 AI 投资从叙事驱动⾛向资本回报验证的重要分⽔岭。未来最⼤的赢家，未必是资本开⽀
最多的公司，⽽可能是那些能够组织最多有效算⼒、承担最少不必要资本⻛险，并持续获取最
⾼⻛险调整后资本回报的企业。

AI ⾰命还在继续。但资本市场已经开始追问⼀个更根本的问题：技术进步创造的价值，究竟能否
⽀付推动这场⾰命所需要的全部资本成本？
本⽂来源：⻉叶斯之美

### Page 8

Document 343: European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United  States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3  according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever -volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2 -0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues . As anticipated by our  Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2).  
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%).  According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2),  driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export gr owth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports ( -1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q).  Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, gr owth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026 , 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate ( AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheles s 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI -related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk -reward in 10 -year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI -
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI -investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short -
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk -reward in US 10 -year government bonds.  
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel -
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer -than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non -farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. W hile some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we  agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries.  US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long -term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold.  The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher -yielding Emerging Market USD and non -USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6 –12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions 
  Do you expect more RBA rate hikes after its less hawkish 
guidance? What is your view on the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We expect the RBA to leave rates unchanged, but 
retain the option to hike again should inflation intensify. We 
maintain our medium-term constructive AUD view and maintain 
our opportunistically bullish AUD corporate bonds idea. 
Rationale: The RBA’s forward guidance remains data dependent, 
explicitly allowing further tightening if needed as inflation remains too 
high.  At 4.60%, Australia’s cash rate is currently 60–85bp above the 
Fed’s 3.75–4.00% range. Thus, even with our two additional 25bp s 
Fed hikes expectation, Australian rates would hold a modest positive 
rate differential. We expect the AUD to strengthen once the Fed 
signals that US rates are near their peak, which should allow resilient 
Australian fundamentals to reassert themselves. Key risks are 
persistently high US yields, weaker China growth or a sharper 
deterioration in global risk sentiment. 
We believe risk-reward has become more attractive for AUD bonds 
following the bear -flattening of the AUD yield curve. Markets are 
already pricing in one more RBA hike through end-2027, presenting 
an attractive opportunity to earn a yield even as increasingly 
restrictive financial conditions reinforc e disinflation over time. 
Meanwhile, the positive interest rate differential with the US supports 
the currency outlook, supporting the case for AUD bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  What does the incoming US data tell us about the path for 
US 10-year bond yields from here? 
Our view: The risk -reward in US 10 -year yields has turned 
attractive following their move higher amid incoming US data. 
Rationale: Recent US data has indeed started to show softer-than-
expected inflation. However, market technicals are likely temporarily 
delaying a move lower in bond yields, with reports suggesting the  
rise in yields is creating forced selling amongst hedge funds and real 
estate investment trusts. Nevertheless, we do not expect this to be 
an extended selloff. We have initiated an opportunistic bullish idea 
on US 10-year government bonds this week and continue to believe 
this idea offers an attractive risk/reward on a 6-12-month horizon as 
yields become attractive enough to entice buyer flow to return. On a 
broader allocation basis, we retain our structural preference for 3-7-
year maturities in bonds. 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds amid 
Australia-US interest rate differentials and positive 
outlook for the AUD

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in the GBP ahead of the UK 
budget announcement next month? 
Our view: We maintain a medium-term constructive GBP view, 
but keep near-term exposure measured ahead of the 28 October 
Budget. We would view any pre -Budget weakness as an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure. 
Rationale: The government’s decision to adjust the pension triple 
lock from 2030 is an early indication of greater willingness to tackle 
longer-term spending pressures, reinforcing Chancellor Healey’s 
commitment to fiscal discipline and meeting the fiscal rules. Markets 
are likely to scrutinise the Budget closely for evidence that this 
discipline extends to near -term tax and spending decisions. A 
credible Budget should help contain pressure on UK government 
bond yields and support GBP. Any fiscal slippage remains a key risk.  
Near term, we prefer measured GBP exposure as elevated US yields 
and repricing towards further Fed tightening could continue to 
support the USD. Over the medium term, we expect GBP to rebound 
as the Fed eventually signals the peak of its tightening cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How would different US mid-term election outcome 
scenarios affect the outlook for US equities? 
Our view: We believe election outcomes will have a limited 
impact over the next 12 months. We remain positive on US 
equities driven by fundamental earnings growth.  
Rationale: Over the past 50 years, the ruling US President’s party 
has retained control of both chambers of Congress on only two 
occasions (1978 and 2002). All other elections result ed in an 
opposition sweep or a divided government.  
Based on the latest prediction markets, it appears that an opposition 
sweep (both the House and Senate flips from Republican to 
Democrats) is more likely than a divided government ( in which the 
House flips to Democrats , while the Senate remains under 
Republican control). A scenario where Republicans retain a majority 
is least likely per prediction markets. 
While mid -term elections may introduce near -term volatility, we 
caution against overreaction. Historically, the average return for the 
S&P 500 following an opposition sweep is 17% over the next 12 
months, but the average return following a divided government is not 
significantly worse, at 15%. We prefer to base our investment views 
on fundamental earnings growth, where investor attention should 
focus on the upcoming Q3 earnings season. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst

GBP/USD’s pre-Budget weakness sets an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure over 
the medium-term

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the outlook for  the memory chip and broader 
semiconductors industry? 
Our view: We remain positive on semiconductors and memory 
chips. Structural AI demand should prolong the memory 
upcycle while long-term agreements improve revenue visibility. 
Rationale: Recent industry commentary points to tighter memory 
supply-demand conditions through 2028, reinforcing our view that AI 
demand is likely to extend the memory upcycle. Furthermore, adoption 
of long-term agreements improves revenue and earnings visibility. 
Despite debates around “Pacing the Frontier”, we have seen a flurry 
of new model releases, along with the introduction of new consumer 
AI agents capable of comparing prices online and making 
purchases. These are early days, but the path is open for a business-
to-agent (B2A) economy where businesses increasingly interact with 
AI assistants acting on customers’ behalf. As these applications are 
increasingly inference intensive, wider adoption should support 
demand for memory, with its share of global semiconductor 
revenues projected to rise from 30.7% in 2025 to 66.7% in 2027. 
Against this backdrop, we maintain our Opportunistic idea on Global 
Semiconductors. Given memory  chips’ cyclical nature, investors 
should avoid overconcentration in memory names and maintain a 
diversified exposure across Big Tech and semiconductors. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Does a US bond yield stress test raise the risk to your 
constructive view on US equities? 
Our view: We have a target for the S&P 500 index of 8400 over 
the next 12 months. A scenario of a sustained rise in the US 10-
year government bond yield to 5.6% could lead to negative 
returns from the current S&P500 level (7,666). 
Rationale: Our base case 12-month target for the S&P 500 is 8,400, 
which assumes 2027 S&P 500 EPS of USD 420 and a 20x forward 
P/E multiple. A rise in bond yields would have an impact on equity 
valuations, as a higher discount rate reduces the present value of 
future earnings. W e stress -test our target under different 10 -year 
yield and earnings scenarios.  
A rise in bond yields would arguably be associated with a stronger 
growth environment and hence, even higher earnings. However, 
assuming unchanged EPS at our base case of USD 420, a 
(sustained) rise in the 10 -year bond yield to 5.6% would lead to 
negative returns from the current level  if not offset by a further 
improvement in earnings growth . However, in o ur base case  we 
expect the 10-year yield is more likely to fall from here. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
The economy has shown resilience in the face of the energy shock: GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3, and the 1.0% growth carry -over for 2026 
supports our forecast of 1.1% for the year as a whole (compared to 1.3% in 2025)  with the risk tilted to the upside.  Growth is expected to 
strengthen to 1.6% in 2027, bolstered by increased spending on defence and infrastructure, as well as the global investment cycle in AI. Annual 
average inflation is thus expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, 
we anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, follo wed by an extended pause. That 
said, stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aeronautics production (dela y in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected  to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027 (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in 2026 and 3. 5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, the BoE has finally raised its Bank Rate to 4.00%; If a second-rate hike looks likely for February, our baseline 
scenario projects that easing will resume in the second half of 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate down to 3.50% by year‑end.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026

### Page 2

Photo by Baptiste Azais / Unsplash
The stock market dived last week. Investors priced in political backlash
against data centers after Democrats swept Congress. We anticipated
buying opportunities after the midterm elections, and Thursday’s selloff
delivered some. This remains an earnings-led bull market. Valuations are
falling while earnings are soaring. We still view the AI backlash as another

### Page 3

speed bump, not a roadblock. We are sticking with our 7,900 year-end
target for the S&P 500.
I. Performance
The Nasdaq entered correction territory on Thursday and closed the week
below its 200-day moving average (chart). It has done this before recently.
It rebounded sharply after its Spring 2025 "Liberation Day" plunge and
again after the Iran war started earlier this year, quickly resuming its
uptrend both times.
The equal-weight S&P 500 is holding up remarkably well. It remains near

its recent record high and above both its 50-day and 200-day moving
averages (chart). The damage has been concentrated in the AI trade, not
the broader market.

### Page 4

The Magnificent-7 are underperforming again. The MAGS ETF is up only
2.4% ytd, versus 7.8% for the S&P 500 (chart). The Impressive-493 also
took a hit this week. The XMAGS ETF includes Micron and the other
semiconductor stocks hit hardest in the selloff. It is still up 10.0% ytd.
Dow Theory remains bullish. The Dow Jones Industrial Average is holding
up well near its highs (chart). The Transports have lagged. They should
improve if oil prices continue to fall.

II. Earnings
Earnings season picked up this week. The blended Q3 S&P 500 EPS
growth estimate (i.e., actual results for companies that have reported,
estimates for those that haven’t yet) rose to 25.9% y/y from 23.8% a week
earlier, helped by AMD’s solid results. Q4’s estimate edged down to 29.7%

### Page 5

(chart). As we noted previously, Q2’s 50.4% y/y EPS growth rate was
boosted by mark-to-market gains at Alphabet and Amazon. Excluding
those, growth was closer to 25%, which is still very strong.
Forward earnings rose to a record $418.25. It is converging on the 2027
estimate of $426.28, which continues to climb (chart). Analysts’ 2028
consensus S&P 500 EPS estimate rose to $495.14; it’s been closing in on
$500—which is our target for 2030!  We are bullish, but not as bullish as

the analysts. The drop in the forward P/E this year suggests that investors
also believe analysts are too bullish on earnings.
Forward earnings for the S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 all rose to
record highs again last week (chart). Earnings strength remains broad.

### Page 6

S&P 500 forward revenues per share is up 15.9% y/y, while forward
earnings per share is up 39.0% y/y (chart).
III. Valuation
The S&P 500 forward P/E fell to 17.6 last week, below the 2025 Liberation
Day low (chart). P/E plunges typically accompany corrections and
recessions. The economy shows no sign of a recession anytime soon.
Investors instead fear irrational exuberance in earnings forecasts.

### Page 7

Since the bull market began on October 12, 2022, the S&P 500 has risen
106.2%. Forward earnings is up 75.5%, while the forward P/E is up 22.5%
(chart). The P/E and E both drove the rally until the forward P/E peaked in
October last year. Since then, E has done all the work. Investors are
finding it harder to predict the return on all the AI capital spending. They
won’t pay an excessive multiple given that uncertainty.
The S&P 500 Semiconductors industry accounts for much of the P/E drop.

The industry index's forward P/E has plunged to 13.1, well below the S&P
500’s (chart). Semis are now cheaper than they were during the 2022
bear market.

### Page 8

IV. Fixed Income
The futures market expects about two rate hikes over the next 12 months.
That explains why the 2-year Treasury yield remains well above the 3.88%
federal funds rate, even after easing to 4.46% following the midterms
(chart).
The 10-year Treasury yield continues to fall. It dropped to 4.84% and is
testing its uptrend line from last year’s low (chart). We’ll see if it bounces.
Either way, it is back in the 4.00%-5.00% “old normal” range where we feel

comfortable.

### Page 9

The 10-year TIPS yield fell to 2.62%. The yield spread between the 10-
year Treasury and TIPS, a widely used proxy for expected inflation, eased
to 2.22% (chart). That proxy tends to move with oil prices. The recent drop
in crude showed up here.
V. Commodities
The price of Brent crude oil fell to $98.32 a barrel in recent days (chart).
No news of the re-escalation of the war in the Middle East has emerged
since the midterms. Hopes for a US-Iran deal are reflected in the oil

market. We still see a risk of re-escalation.

### Page 10

Nearby gasoline futures dropped to $2.78 a gallon last week (chart). If
sustained, that should start to show up as lower inflation in a couple of
months. Renewed fighting in the Middle East would reverse that.
Steel prices show no sign of a slowdown in data center construction. US
Midwest hot-rolled coil rose to $1,357 a ton, the highest since 2022
(chart).

### Page 11

Agricultural prices have eased. The S&P GSCI Agriculture Spot index fell
to 376.77 last week (chart). Fears of a food price spike should subside.

Document 455: Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Why hasn’t there been a major financial crisis in recent years_ (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 19
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Why hasn’t there been a major 
financial crisis in recent years?

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

Peter Sidorov, CFA 
Senior Economist

Camilla Siazon 
Research Analyst

November 3, 2026 
— The economist Hyman Minsky warned, in essence, that “stability is destabilising” . He 
argued that a lengthy period of financial stability could be problematic, as it induced 
risk-taking and complacency amongst investors, laying the foundation for the next 
crisis. For example, lenders relax their terms and borrowers are more willing to extend 
themselves, as nobody wants to miss out. This dynamic makes economies inherently 
prone to financial panics and boom-bust cycles. 
— But since the 2008 crisis, and the subsequent sovereign crisis in the Eurozone, we 
have yet to see another financial crisis happen on a scale that had a lasting market 
and macroeconomic impact. There have been clear moments of concern, as in early 
2023 with the regional bank turmoil, but nothing that has caused a lasting impact. So 
this led us to ask why. 
— In this paper, we examine why the post-GFC era has seen fewer financial crises. 
Explanations include the reforms made after 2008 to avoid a repeat, the lower 
proportion of financial assets held by the banking system, as well as the speedier 
reaction of policymakers when wobbles have occurred. We then consider a few 
candidates that could cause the next crisis, including AI vulnerabilities, private credit, 
and stretched fiscal positions. 
— Ultimately, financial crises haven’t been abolished, but the reforms of recent years 
have made it harder for shocks to propagate through the traditional banking system. 
Moreover, policymakers have become far more inclined to intervene when initial signs 
of stress have emerged.

### Page 2

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 1: What is a financial crisis, and what causes them?
There isn’t a single technical definition of a “financial crisis”, and they come in many
different types. Commonly, we think of banking crises like 2008, but they can also
encompass currency/balance of payments crises, debt crises and other forms too.
Given currency and sovereign crises have been more common in emerging markets
(the UK's ERM crisis in 1992 and the euro crisis being the two most notable DM

occurrences), historical analysis in developed economies has tended to focus
heavily on banking crises. Non-bank funding or market-liquidity crises are often not
categorised separately in the literature, but they have become more common in
recent years. This includes the "dash-for-cash" stress at the onset of the Covid
pandemic in March 2020 and the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK.
But for all the different types of financial crisis, there are common themes between

them. Often, they are accompanied by asset bubbles that burst in a non-linear
fashion. We generally see a severe tightening in credit conditions, which in turn
flows through into a broader economic contraction. Frequently, there are
contagion-like dynamics that can be difficult to stop. And in the modern world, they
usually require policymakers to step in, stabilising the situation with interventions.
At risk of oversimplification, we note four factors that determine the emergence and

the severity of a financial crisis. The first three are well-established in the financial
literature, sometimes pooled together into a build-up and a crisis phase
(Brunnermeier & Oehmke 2013), while the fourth has become increasingly
important over the past 15-20 years:
1.  Underlying imbalances. Bubbles and financial imbalances build during
the run-up phase, leaving accumulated risks that materialise when the
crisis erupts. This is the build-up typically described by the early phases of

Kindleberger's model of financial crises.
2.  Trigger. This is the shock that turns an existing vulnerability into clear
financial stress. These are often very difficult to predict and we mostly leave
these outside the focus of this report.
3.  Amplification and financial system fragility. Various factors determine
whether a shock propagates through the financial system, and the
intensity of that shock. These include leverage, liquidity and maturity

mismatches, interconnectedness, and the concentration of exposures.
Other channels in the financial system can then amplify the shock,
including bank runs, fire sales and margin calls, and negative net worth
effects, sovereign–bank loops etc.
4.  Intervention/policy response. This has been increasingly important in
recent decades as the size and scope of policy response to crises has
increased. The outright size can vary, and intervention can occur across

several dimensions, ranging from direct intervention in financial markets,
to counter-cyclical macroeconomic policy that mitigates the impact on the
real economy.
Clearly, these factors are not independent and the interaction between them is what
determines the severity of a financial crisis.

### Page 3

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
One important aspect is the joint role that underlying imbalances and amplification
play. While theoretical models such as Diamond & Dybvig highlight the potential for
a bank run that triggers a broad crisis, empirical research shows that in practice, it
is exceedingly rare for a bank run to lead to bank failure in the absence of an
underlying solvency issue (Correia, Luck and Verner 2026). In other words, the

presence of amplification channels alone does not lead to a crisis.
Conversely, in the absence of amplification, it is possible to get significant
turbulence in asset prices, but without a financial crisis. For instance, a bear market,
wider credit spreads, or even a recession do not in and of themselves imply there
is a financial crisis. This was seen clearly with the bursting of the dot com bubble,
a severe equity decline but not a financial crisis, though it did cause a recession. At

the same time, in the chart below, we can see several US equity bear markets that
took place outside of recessions. On other occasions, however, a “Minsky
Moment” can then happen, where even a seemingly minor event can trigger a high-
profile incident that cascades across the financial system.
Figure 1: S&P 500 drawdowns over time, with shaded recessions showing select episodes when the index fell over 20%
without coinciding with a recession
Source :Bloomberg Finance LP, NBER, Deutsche Bank Research

Looking back through history, we can see how variation in these factors has led to
different regimes over time in terms of frequency/severity of financial factors.

### Page 4

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 2. Why have financial crises been more or less likely
in certain periods of history?
1. Pre-WWII: More frequent financial instability, albeit under a very
different system.
Financial instability was a recurrent feature of the pre-WWII era. During the 19th
and early 20th centuries, there were frequent panics that often led to major slumps.
For instance, the Panic of 1873 sparked a period often known as the “Long

Depression”, which extended into the 1890s for many countries.
In part, financial panics occurred more frequently because policy operated under
very different settings. For instance, the Gold Standard as the monetary system
constrained a countercyclical policy response and transmitted deflation
internationally, while falling prices increased real debt burdens. Moreover, the
Federal Reserve wasn’t established until 1913, and the lack of an effective lender of

last resort meant there was a private intervention during the US Panic of 1907. The
first wave of globalisation also brought new vulnerabilities. Between 1870 and
1913, many emerging economies accumulated debts denominated in gold or
foreign currency, leaving them exposed when capital inflows reversed or exchange
rates weakened.
The biggest and most famous crisis occurred in 1929, when the Wall Street Crash
of that year sparked the Great Depression. The length and depth of that crisis helped

to stimulate the study of modern macroeconomics, with huge efforts to prevent a
repeat over the years ahead. Indeed, the failures of the response to the Great
Depression played a large role in the much swifter response to the 2008 financial
crisis, helping to avoid a second Great Depression.
That experience established much of the modern financial safety net that exists to
this day. For instance in the US, the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation was

established in 1933. The US and many other countries came off the Gold Standard
as well in this period. There were also regulatory reforms, and the Glass-Steagall Act
separated out commercial from investment banking.
2. 1944-1971: The Bretton Woods era coincided with fewer financial
crises
Around the end of WWII, economic policymakers sought to create an international
monetary system for the post-war era. The relative financial calm that followed

reflected a combination of restrictions on international capital movement, tightly
regulated domestic financial systems, and a system of fixed exchange rates that
aimed to provide stability and prevent the competitive devaluations that occurred
in the 1930s. This saw exchange rates pegged against the US Dollar, which in turn
was convertible to gold at $35 per ounce.
At the same time, capital controls helped preserve monetary policy flexibility, while

maintaining the system of fixed exchange rates. These controls had several
downsides but they also insulated countries from “sudden stop” crises that can
occur, when capital rapidly flees a country. Moreover, capital controls prevented
the rapid buildup of cross-border systemic leverage. So it was more difficult to
generate a global financial crisis in the same way.

### Page 5

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Figure 2: Gold price (USD/oz, log scale)
10
100
1000
1920 1940 1960 1980 2000 2020
In the Bretton Woods
era, the dollar was tied
to gold
Source : Finaeon, Deutsche Bank
Alongside capital controls, there were also domestic reforms in the aftermath of the
Great Depression, which sought to avoid a repeat. As mentioned earlier, the US
Glass-Steagall Act of 1933 separated commercial banking from investment

banking. Another example of the era’s regulated financial system in the US was
Regulation Q, which imposed maximum interest rates that commercial banks
could pay on deposits. That meant depositors could not search for yield and cause
a run on deposits as easily. Another rationale was that with institutions not
competing for deposits, it would avoid a compression in the margin between the
rates banks paid on their lending and borrowing, which would disincentivise a push

into riskier investments.
Finally, economic growth was also stronger in this period, with the period between
WWII and the 1973 oil shock often described as the “Golden Age”. This played a role
in avoiding financial crises too, as faster growth helped keep debt default rates
down, avoiding the kind of “trigger” events above that might spark a financial crisis.
Coupled with financial repression, strong growth also helped to bring down the
high post-war public debt burdens.

Over time however, the peg between the dollar and gold found itself under growing
strain. The economist Robert Triffin identified the “Triffin dilemma”. Supplying the
world with dollar reserves resulted in outstanding dollar liabilities around the world
that exceeded the gold reserves in the US, weakening confidence that dollars could
ultimately be redeemed for gold. Conversely, restricting dollar supply to protect
those reserves and preserve convertibility risked depriving the system of the

liquidity needed to support trade and growth.
These tensions intensified in the late 1960s amid expansionary US policies,
including spending on the Vietnam War and Great Society programmes. The
continued drain in US gold reserves eventually led the US to suspend the
convertibility of the US Dollar into gold, effectively ending the Bretton Woods
system. Initially, there were attempts to bring some kind of fixed-exchange rate
system back, but the world of fiat currencies (with no link to a precious metal)

persists to this day.

### Page 6

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
3. 1971-2008: Financial crises become more frequent after the
breakdown of Bretton Woods to the Global Financial Crisis
After the breakdown of the Bretton Woods system, financial crises became a more
regular occurrence. While the end of the gold convertibility did not by itself trigger
future crises, it opened the door for wider financial liberalisation, with the resulting
rise in capital mobility and looser domestic financial regulation then helping to

facilitate a global build-up of imbalances.
The 1980s and 1990s were a period of frequent crisis events. The transition away
from capital controls meant that capital could flow more freely across borders.
However, this could also cause a “sudden stop” if investors suddenly pulled their
capital out of a country, contributing to bank runs and currency collapses.
Moreover, many emerging markets borrowed in foreign currencies like the US
Dollar, meaning that a depreciation in their own currency would see this USD-

denominated debt surge in local currency terms. This meant it was a particular
problem if US interest rates were increasing, as it became harder for many
emerging markets to repay their debts. This was a contributing factor behind the
Latin American debt crisis in the early 1980s. And the rate hikes also had domestic
ramifications, being a contributing factor behind the Savings and Loan crisis in the
United States.
With capital flowing more freely, that also played a role in the 1994 Mexican Peso

Crisis, and the 1997 Asian Financial Crisis. A sudden reversal in capital flows led to
crisis and ultimately recession in many countries. The following year, the Russian
financial crisis then took place, and fears grew in the United States about the wider
ramifications, as the hedge fund Long-Term Capital Management (LTCM) came
under pressure. Indeed, this period saw 75bps of pre-emptive rate cuts from the
Federal Reserve.
This volatility also saw the Fed proactively seek to step in and smooth out the

economic volatility. This was broadly successful, but in the process also helped
engender complacency and a further buildup of leverage. Indeed, after the dot com
bubble burst, the federal funds rate was cut to just 1% in mid-2003, where it
remained until mid-2004.
This period saw clear optimism that another Great Depression was highly unlikely.
For instance, Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Lucas argued in his 2003 AEA
Presidential Address that “macroeconomics in this original sense has succeeded:

Its central problem of depression prevention has been solved, for all practical
purposes, and has in fact been solved for many decades.”
However, as we now know, the 2008 financial crisis occurred, driving the biggest
global downturn since the Great Depression of the 1930s. It turned out that Minsky
had been correct, and a lengthy period without a crisis had once again helped to
induce complacency, laying the foundations for a repeat.

### Page 7

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
Figure 3: DM economies have avoided systemic financial
crises since the post-GFC period
Source : Laeven and Valencia (2026).
Number of financial crises by year since 1970 (by start date)
Figure 4: Banking crises were very uncommon in the post-
WWII period, but then proliferated after the 1970s
Source : Reinhart and Rogoff (2013)
Countries with banking and external debt crises, weighted by their share of world income, 1900-2008

Section 3: The Post-2008 world: Why have we not had
another big financial crisis?
Since the Global Financial Crisis of 2008, we have thankfully avoided a major
financial crisis. Despite clear moments of stress, most notably around the collapse
of SVB in March 2023, we have not seen a similar wave of bank collapses on that
scale. There are several components to this.
First, post-GFC deleveraging cleared out risks in the system, with countries and
sectors that had excess indebtedness before the GFC experiencing pronounced

declines in leverage.
Second, there have been reforms explicitly designed to avoid a repeat of 2008.
After the crisis happened, there was a clear recognition that gaps in regulation had
exacerbated the crisis, leading to effects that cascaded across the financial system.
For instance, the Basel III reforms required banks to hold more equity, with a
minimum requirement for Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital. They also
introduced the liquidity coverage ratio, which requires banks to hold enough high-

quality liquid assets to ensure they can cover their net cash outflows under a 30-day
stressed scenario. In addition, the net stable funding ratio was brought in, requiring
banks to have sufficient stable funding that exceeded the required amount of stable
funding for a one-year stressed scenario.
At the national level, further reforms were also introduced. In the US, the Dodd-
Frank Act was introduced, with measures including the Volcker Rule that restricted

banks from engaging in proprietary trading, and required the Federal Reserve to
perform an annual stress test of large institutions. Meanwhile in the European
Union, which also experienced a sovereign debt crisis, reforms included a “Single
Supervisory Mechanism”, whereby banking supervision was brought under the
ECB, ensuring harmonised regulatory standards.

### Page 8

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
Third, the share of financial assets held by banks has fallen since the 2008 crisis.
This hasn’t eliminated risk, but commercial banks are funded by retail deposits that
can quickly fly. By contrast, non-bank financial institutions (NBFIs), e.g. hedge
funds, are not as prone to these bank run dynamics. For instance, a private credit
closed-ended fund is not one where investors can simply redeem their funds on

demand.
We can see this shift in the data. Looking at the FSB’s group of 21 plus the Eurozone,
we can see that the share of assets held by banks has fallen from a peak of 46% in
2008, to 38% in 2024. By contrast, the share held by NBFIs has risen from 43% to
51%.
Figure 5: Share of Assets of Financial Corporations (FSB 21 + EA Group)
35%
40%
45%
50%
2002 2006 2010 2014 2018 2022
Banks Nonbank Financial Intermediation Sector (NBFI)
Source :Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank

Fourth, the lessons of 2008 have meant that when the financial system has had
wobbles since, policymakers have acted with incredible speed to stop the
turmoil.
This was most obvious when the Covid-19 pandemic began. Whilst a pandemic
was very different to a financial crisis, there was initially huge turmoil in financial
markets. Indeed, the S&P 500 fell by more than a third in just over a month, a decline
at a speed and scale unseen since the Great Depression. Meanwhile, US HY credit

spreads soared to levels last seen around the financial crisis, and at the height of the
turmoil, there was a huge spike in real yields, despite the economy entering a
recession.
Against this backdrop, central banks wasted no time in acting. For instance, the Fed
delivered an emergency 50bp rate cut, followed by another 100bp rate cut.
Moreover, they swiftly resumed Quantitative Easing, and announced the
establishment of multiple new facilities to ensure the smooth functioning of credit

markets. These included a Primary Market Corporate Credit Facility, to support new
bond and loan issuance, along with a Secondary Market Corporate Credit facility,
which offered liquidity for outstanding corporate bonds. Meanwhile, a third facility,
called the Term Asset-Backed Securities Loan Facility, was set up to enable the
issuance of asset-backed securities. Similarly in Europe, the ECB set up a new
Pandemic Emergency Purchase Programme (PEPP), which ultimately had a total

envelope of €1.85tn.
Whilst the pandemic caused a huge economic shock, the swift action meant it did
not translate into a financial one, and the brief tightening in credit conditions was

### Page 9

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
unwound over the coming months.
A similar pattern was seen during the regional banking turmoil of March 2023. This
was a financial panic, which initially began with the collapse of Silicon Valley Bank.
However, this began to spread, and Signature Bank also failed. Then in Europe,
Credit Suisse came under investor scrutiny and saw large deposit outflows, which
culminated in a purchase by UBS that included guarantees from the Swiss

government. This coincided with huge market moves, with the 2yr Treasury yield
posting its biggest daily decline in over 40 years, with a 61bps decline on March 13.
However, policymakers swiftly moved to stop any contagion. The US Treasury,
Federal Reserve and the FDIC released a joint statement stating that all deposits at
SVB would be protected, and that the Federal Reserve would provide additional
funding to ensure banks could meet the needs of depositors. Meanwhile, the Fed’s

balance sheet briefly expanded again, despite the fact it had begun the process of
quantitative tightening. These actions ultimately meant the crisis proved contained
and did not lead to a wave of further bank collapses in the manner of 2008.
Figure 6: Federal Reserve Total Assets (USD trillions)
0

GFC Covid-19
SVB
Source : Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank
Fifth, policymakers have also responded to recent shocks with huge fiscal

transfers, meaning that debt has migrated from private towards sovereign
balance sheets.
Whilst global debt has risen slightly over the last decade, if you dig deeper into the
numbers, that’s entirely driven by a rise in government debt. If we compare the
statistics for Q2 2026, relative to Q2 2016, there’s actually been a decline in debt for
households, financial corporations, and nonfinancial corporations. The only one of
the four segments that’s gone up in the last decade is government debt.

In relative terms, the transformation is even bigger if we look back to 2007, before
the Global Financial Crisis kicked off. Back then, government debt was the smallest
of the four categories, but because of the GFC and the pandemic, it’s now become
the biggest of the four categories.
That in turn has led to separate issues, with government bond yields at multi-year
highs, alongside rising net interest spending. So the fiscal space has become
limited. However, public balance sheets don’t face the same kind of system-wide

run that a bank would.
